╭──────────────────── logging - explorer - system_message ─────────────────────╮
│                                                                              │
│                                   Context                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • You are a helpful agent part of a Multi Agent System that solves server   │
│    and command-line related tasks.                                           │
│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │
│    summaries, and information from previous agents, such as previous         │
│    sub-task plans, feedback, executed steps, exploration details and more.   │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Role: you are the Explorer agent of the Multi Agent System.               │
│  • Based on the provided task by the user, use the given tools to gather     │
│    some basic information relevant to completing the task.                   │
│  • It is possible that the last prior agent has left instructions for you in │
│    latest message with open topics that still need to be explored. If so,    │
│    prioritize answering the other agents' exploration requests.              │
│  • Do not try to solve the tasks, instead, collect only basic information    │
│    that will assist later agents in planning and actively solving the task.  │
│  • When finished write a concise summary of only the gathered information    │
│    and include the tag '[subtaskplanner]'.                                   │
│  • Include a tag in your answer only when you intend to pass it on.          │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                         Code Execution Capabilities                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your are equipped with different tools for executing code.                │
│  • Do only use the tools to execute code. Code inside a Markdown Code Block  │
│    will not be executed.                                                     │
│  • Use Shell tool for simple, single bash commands. Do not use it for        │
│    commands that open interactive terminal programs (e.g. git log or man).   │
│  • Use Terminal tool to execute commands in a persistent terminal instance   │
│    and get back the exact terminal screen. This is especially useful for     │
│    interactive programs such as git log, man, or vim.                        │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                             More Operating Rules                             │
│                                                                              │
│  • Think and act step by step and explain your actions and reasoning.        │
│  • Break complex objectives into smaller sub-goals if necessary.             │
│  • Use tools to gather knowledge or verify assumptions before proceeding.    │
│  • If you call a tool it will be executed and results will be returned to    │
│    you.                                                                      │
╰──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯
╭───────────────── logging - subtaskplanner - system_message ──────────────────╮
│                                                                              │
│                                   Context                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • You are a helpful agent part of a Multi Agent System that solves server   │
│    and command-line related tasks.                                           │
│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │
│    summaries, and information from previous agents, such as previous         │
│    sub-task plans, feedback, executed steps, exploration details and more.   │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Role: you are the Subtaskplanner agent of the Multi Agent System.         │
│  • Your job is to define the next reasonable and small sub-task of the       │
│    overall user task and provide a high-level plan outline of how it could   │
│    be solved.                                                                │
│  • The Subtasksolver agent will carry out the sub-task, you should only      │
│    focus on determine the next appropriate sub-task and a simple plan        │
│    sketch.                                                                   │
│  • Make sure the sub-task and plan sketch describe only the next reasonable  │
│    step forward, keeping in mind that some work may already have been        │
│    completed by previous agents.                                             │
│  • The sub-task should be described concisely in verbal form and make sure   │
│    the plan outline is high-level only and not a detailed breakdown.         │
│  • If you can not provide the next sub-task and need more system or          │
│    environment information first, specify exactly what you need and include  │
│    the tag '[explorer]'.                                                     │
│  • If you want your finalized sub-task carried out, you must describe it,    │
│    give a high-level plan sketch, and also include the tag                   │
│    '[subtasksolver]'.                                                        │
│  • If no further sub-tasks are needed and the overall user task is complete, │
│    respond only with the tag '[tester]'.                                     │
│  • Include a tag in your answer only when you intend to pass it on.          │

╭────────────────── logging - subtasksolver - system_message ──────────────────╮
│                                                                              │
│                                   Context                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • You are a helpful agent part of a Multi Agent System that solves server   │
│    and command-line related tasks.                                           │
│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │
│    summaries, and information from previous agents, such as previous         │
│    sub-task plans, feedback, executed steps, exploration details and more.   │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Role: you are the Subtasksolver agent of the Multi Agent System.          │
│  • Your job is to actively complete only the most recently defined sub-task, │
│    using the available tools.                                                │
│  • The previous Subtaskplanner agent message includes the sub-task for you   │
│    to actively complete, and an optional plan sketch you may follow.         │
│  • Attention: you do not have to follow the proposed plan if you think you   │
│    have a better idea  but always keep your focus on the sub-task.           │
│  • Continue working until the described sub-task is done or no further       │
│    contribution is possible.                                                 │
│  • Also, verify whether the chosen sub-task is reasonable. If it needs       │
│    refinement, provide your feedback and include the tag '[subtaskplanner]'. │
│  • When finished, or you get stuck, write a summary of what you did and      │
│    include the tag '[subtaskplanner]'.                                       │
│  • Include a tag in your answer only when you intend to pass it on.          │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                         Code Execution Capabilities                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your are equipped with different tools for executing code.                │
│  • Do only use the tools to execute code. Code inside a Markdown Code Block  │
│    will not be executed.                                                     │
│  • Use Shell tool for simple, single bash commands. Do not use it for        │
│    commands that open interactive terminal programs (e.g. git log or man).   │
│  • Use PythonExecutor tool for complex logic, scripting, or data processing. │
│  • Use Terminal tool to execute commands in a persistent terminal instance   │
│    and get back the exact terminal screen. This is especially useful for     │
│    interactive programs such as git log, man, or vim.                        │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                             More Operating Rules                             │
│                                                                              │
│  • Think and act step by step and explain your actions and reasoning.        │
│  • Break complex objectives into smaller sub-goals if necessary.             │
│  • Use tools to gather knowledge or verify assumptions before proceeding.    │
│  • If you call a tool it will be executed and results will be returned to    │
│    you.                                                                      │

│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your role is the "Approver" agent of the Multi Agent System.              │
│  • Your job is to determine if the agents have done enough work on the task. │
│  • Make your decision based on all information gathered and the actions      │
│    performed by previous agents.                                             │
│  • Be critical and selective, approve only what you are completely sure of.  │
│  • If you decide that the task was not solved successfully, briefly explain  │
│    why and include the tag '[refiner]'.                                      │
│  • If you decide that not enough testing was done to ensure the task is      │
│    fully and successfully completed, briefly explain why and add the tag     │
│    '[tester]'.                                                               │
│  • If you decide that sufficient work was done, just reply briefly with the  │
│    tag '[summarizer]'.                                                       │
│  • Make one of the three decisions and include its tag in your response.     │

│ <output> # Notes On Compiling SQLite On All Kinds Of Unix                    │
│                                                                              │
│ Here are step-by-step instructions on how to build SQLite from canonical     │
│ source on any modern machine that isn't Windows.  These notes are tested (on │
│ 2024-10-11) on Ubuntu and on MacOS, but they are general and should work on  │
│ most any modern unix platform. See the companion document () for guidance on │
│ building for Windows.                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│  1 Install a C-compiler.  GCC or Clang both work fine.  If you are reading   │
│    this document, you've probably already done that.                         │
│  2 (Optional): Install TCL development libraries.  In this note, we'll do a  │
│    private install in the $HOME/local directory, but you can make            │
│    adjustments to install TCL wherever you like. This document assumes you   │
│    are working with TCL version 9.0. See also the  document that contains    │
│    more details on compiling Tcl for use with SQLite. <ol type="a"> <li>Get  │
│    the TCL source archive, perhaps from                                      │
│    <https://www.tcl.tk/software/tcltk/download.html> or                      │
│    <https://sqlite.org/tmp/tcl9.0.0.tar.gz>. <li>Untar the source archive.   │
│    CD into the "unix/" subfolder of the source tree. <li>Run: mkdir          │
│    $HOME/local <li>Run: ./configure --prefix=$HOME/local <li>Run: make       │
│    install </ol> <p> As of 2024-10-25, TCL is not longer required for many   │
│    common build targets, such as "sqlite3.c" or the "sqlite3" command-line   │
│    tool.  So you can skip this step if that is all you want to build.  TCL   │
│    is still required to run "make test" and similar, or to build the TCL     │
│    extension, of course.                                                     │
│  3 Download the SQLite source tree and unpack it. CD into the toplevel       │
│    directory of the source tree.                                             │
│  4 Run: ./configure --enable-all --with-tclsh=$HOME/local/bin/tclsh9.0       │
│    You do not need to use --with-tclsh if the tclsh you want to use is the   │
│    first one on your PATH or if you are building without TCL.                │
│  5 Run the "Makefile" makefile with an appropriate target. Examples: <ul>    │
│    <li>  make sqlite3.c <li>  make sqlite3 <li>  make sqldiff <li>  make     │
│    sqlite3_rsync </ul> <p>None of the targets above require TCL.  TCL is     │
│    needed for the following targets: <ul> <li>  make tclextension-install    │
│    <li>  make devtest <li>  make releasetest <li>  make sqlite3_analyzer     │
│    </ul>                                                                     │
│    It is not required that you run the "tclextension-install" target prior   │
│    to running tests.  However, the tests will run more smoothly if you do.   │
│    The version of SQLite used for the TCL extension does not need to         │
│    correspond to the version of SQLite under test.  So you can install the   │
│    SQLite TCL extension once, and then use it to test many different         │
│    versions of SQLite.                                                       │
│  6 For a debugging build of the CLI, where the ".treetrace" and              │
│    ".wheretrace" commands work, add the the --with-debug argument to         │
│    configure. </output>                                                      │

│ ┃      intended to hold configure code which is specific to the given      ┃ │

│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ proc sqlite-configure {buildMode configScript} { proj-assert                 │
│ {$::sqliteConfig(build-mode) eq "unknown"}                                   │
│ "sqlite-configure must not be called more than once" set allBuildModes       │
│ {canonical autoconf} if {$buildMode ni $allBuildModes} { user-error "Invalid │
│ build mode: $buildMode. Expecting one of: $allBuildModes" } if               │
│ {$::sqliteConfig(all-flag-default)} { set allFlagHelp "Disable these         │
│ extensions: $::sqliteConfig(all-flag-enables)" } else { set allFlagHelp      │
│ "Enable these extensions: $::sqliteConfig(all-flag-enables)" }               │
│                                                                              │
│ set ::sqliteConfig(build-mode) $buildMode                                    │

│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃       which defaults to true is, in the --help text, rendered as:        ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃              --disable-json          Disable JSON functions              ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃   Whereas a bool flag which defaults to false will instead render as:    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                              --enable-FLAG                               ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃   Non-boolean flags, in contrast, use the names specifically given to    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃   them in the [options] invocation. e.g. "with-tcl" is the --with-tcl    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                  flag.                                   ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                        Fetching values for flags:                        ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                          booleans: use one of:                           ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    - [opt-bool FLAG] is autosetup's built-in command for this, but we    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                     have some convenience variants:                      ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                         - [proj-opt-truthy FLAG]                         ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                - [proj-opt-if-truthy FLAG {THEN} {ELSE}]                 ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                     Non-boolean (i.e. string) flags:                     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                        - [opt-val FLAG ?default?]                        ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃         - [opt-str ...] - see the docs in ./autosetup/autosetup          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃   [proj-opt-was-provided] can be used to determine whether a flag was    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    explicitly provided, which is often useful for distinguishing from    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                       the case of a default value.                       ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ######################################################################## set │
│ allFlags { # Structure: a list of M {Z} pairs, where M is a descriptive #    │
│ option group name  and Z is a list of X Y pairs. X is a list of # $buildMode │
│ name(s) to which the Y flags apply, or {*} to apply # to all builds. Y is a  │
│ {block} in the form expected by # autosetup's [options] command.  Each block │
│ which is applicable # to $buildMode is appended to a new list before that    │
│ list is # passed on to [options]. The order of each Y and sub-Y is #         │
│ retained, which is significant for rendering of --help.                      │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  # When writing {help text blocks}, be aware that:                           │
│  #                                                                           │
│  # A) autosetup formats them differently if the {block} starts with          │
│  # a newline: it starts left-aligned, directly under the --flag, and         │
│  # the rest of the block is pasted verbatim rather than                      │
│  # pretty-printed.                                                           │
│  #                                                                           │
│  # B) Vars and commands are NOT expanded, but we use a [subst] call          │
│  # below which will replace (only) var refs.                                 │
│                                                                              │
│  # Options for how to build the library                                      │
│  build-modes {                                                               │
│    {canonical autoconf} {                                                    │
│      shared=1             =&gt; {Disable build of shared library}            │
│      static=1             =&gt; {Disable build of static library}            │
│    }                                                                         │
│    {canonical} {                                                             │
│      amalgamation=1       =&gt; {Disable the amalgamation and instead build  │
│  all files separately}                                                       │
│    }                                                                         │
│  }                                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│  # Library-level features and defaults                                       │
│  lib-features {                                                              │
│    {*} {                                                                     │
│      threadsafe=1         =&gt; {Disable mutexing}                           │
│      with-tempstore:=no   =&gt; {Use an in-RAM database for temporary        │
│  tables: never,no,yes,always}                                                │
│      load-extension=1     =&gt; {Disable loading of external extensions}     │
│      # ^^^ one of the downstream custom builds overrides the load-extension  │
│  default to 0, which                                                         │
│      # confuses the --help text generator.                                   │
│  https://github.com/msteveb/autosetup/issues/77                              │
│      math=1               =&gt; {Disable math functions}                     │
│      json=1               =&gt; {Disable JSON functions}                     │
│      memsys5              =&gt; {Enable MEMSYS5}                             │
│      memsys3              =&gt; {Enable MEMSYS3}                             │
│      fts3                 =&gt; {Enable the FTS3 extension}                  │
│      fts4                 =&gt; {Enable the FTS4 extension}                  │
│      fts5                 =&gt; {Enable the FTS5 extension}                  │
│      update-limit         =&gt; {Enable the UPDATE/DELETE LIMIT clause}      │
│      geopoly              =&gt; {Enable the GEOPOLY extension}               │
│      rtree                =&gt; {Enable the RTREE extension}                 │
│      session              =&gt; {Enable the SESSION extension}               │
│      all=$::sqliteConfig(all-flag-default) =&gt; {$allFlagHelp}              │
│      largefile=1                                                             │
│        =&gt; {This legacy flag has no effect on the library but may          │
│  influence                                                                   │
│            the generated sqlite_cfg.h by adding #define HAVE_LFS}            │
│    }                                                                         │
│  }                                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│  # Options for TCL support                                                   │
│  tcl {                                                                       │
│    {canonical} {                                                             │
│      tcl=1                                                                   │
│        =&gt; {Disable components which require TCL, including all tests.     │
│            This tree requires TCL for code generation but can use the        │
│  in-tree                                                                     │
│            copy of autosetup/jimsh0.c for that. The SQLite TCL extension     │
│  and the                                                                     │
│            test code require a canonical tclsh.}                             │
│    }                                                                         │
│    {canonical} {                                                             │
│      with-tcl:DIR                                                            │
│        =&gt; {Directory containing tclConfig.sh or a directory one level up  │
│  from                                                                        │
│            that, from which we can derive a directory containing             │
│  tclConfig.sh.                                                               │
│            A dir name of &quot;prefix&quot; is equivalent to the directory   │
│  specified by                                                                │
│            the --prefix flag.}                                               │
│      with-tclsh:PATH                                                         │
│        =&gt; {Full pathname of tclsh to use.  It is used for (A) trying to   │
│  find                                                                        │
│            tclConfig.sh and (B) all TCL-based code generation.  Warning: if  │
│            its containing dir has multiple tclsh versions, it may select     │
│  the                                                                         │
│            wrong tclConfig.sh!}                                              │
│    }                                                                         │
│    {canonical} {                                                             │
│      static-tclsqlite3=0                                                     │
│        =&gt; {Statically-link tclsqlite3. This only works if TCL support is  │
│            enabled and all requisite libraries are available in              │
│            static form. Note that glibc is unable to fully statically        │
│            link certain libraries required by tclsqlite3, so this            │
│  won&#x27;t                                                                  │
│            work on most Linux environments.}                                 │
│    }                                                                         │
│  }                                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│  # Options for line-editing modes for the CLI shell                          │
│  line-editing {                                                              │
│    {canonical autoconf} {                                                    │
│      readline=1                                                              │
│        =&gt; {Disable readline support}                                      │
│      # --with-readline-lib is a backwards-compatible alias for               │
│      # --with-readline-ldflags                                               │
│      with-readline-lib:                                                      │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ </output>                                                                    │

│ ┃    $(CFLAGS) is problematic because it is frequently overridden when     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    invoking make, which loses things like -fPIC. So... we avoid using    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃     it directly and instead add a level of indirection.  We combine      ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃     $(CFLAGS) and $(CPPFLAGS) here because that's the way the legacy     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃     build did it and many builds rely on that. See main.mk for more      ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┃                                 details.                                 ┃ │

│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃       Historical note: the pre-3.48 build only honored CPPFLAGS at       ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃   configure-time, and expanded them into the generated Makefile. There   ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    are, in that build, no uses of CPPFLAGS in the configure-expanded     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    Makefile. Ergo: if a client configures with CPPFLAGS=... and then     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃        explicitly passes CFLAGS=... to make, the CPPFLAGS will be        ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                lost. That behavior is retained in 3.48+.                 ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│       prefix is possible.                                                    │
│  • How to enable gcov in this SQLite tree                                    │
│     • The autosetup/configure driver supports a --gcov flag (configure       │
│       --help shows --gcov).                                                  │
│     • The build templates include gcov-specific variables:                   │
│        • CFLAGS.gcov1 = -DSQLITE_COVERAGE_TEST=1 -fprofile-arcs              │
│          -ftest-coverage                                                     │
│        • LDFLAGS.gcov1 = -lgcov                                              │
│        • USE_GCOV variable controls inclusion; T.compile.gcov and            │
│          T.link.gcov pick these up when USE_GCOV is enabled.                 │
│     • Therefore, running configure with --gcov will set up the makefiles so  │
│       compilation and linking include the necessary gcov flags.              │
│  • Typical build/install targets (from docs and Makefiles)                   │
│     • Common build targets: make sqlite3 (build the CLI), make sqlite3.c     │
│       (generate amalgamated sqlite3.c).                                      │
│     • Installation: configure default prefix is /usr/local; make install     │
│       will install binaries into bindir (e.g., /usr/local/bin) unless a      │
│       different --prefix is passed to configure.                             │
│     • Tests and some build-time code generation rely on tclsh; however       │
│       building the CLI (sqlite3) does not require TCL. The configure output  │
│       notes it can bootstrap a jimsh if canonical tclsh is not present.      │
│  • Noted caveats / useful details for the build step                         │
│     • The Makefile.in contains a comment about potential build/link issues   │
│       when using -fprofile-arcs -ftest-coverage and explains special         │
│       handling via T.compile.gcov / T.link.gcov. The configured makefiles    │
│       handle this when --gcov is used, but keep in mind linking may add      │
│       -lgcov (LDFLAGS.gcov1).                                                │
│     • If making the CLI only, tcl is not required. If tests or certain       │
│       extensions are needed, tcldev may be required.                         │
│     • You can choose an install prefix that is already in PATH and writable  │
│       (e.g., /opt/venv or /usr/local) so the installed sqlite3 becomes       │
│       immediately available.                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ Suggested (later-agent) plan outline for compilation (for the implementer —  │
│ I did not execute these)                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│  • Configure makefiles: cd /app/sqlite && ./configure --prefix=/opt/venv     │
│    --gcov [--disable-tcl]                                                    │
│     • Rationale: --gcov enables coverage flags; --prefix=/opt/venv installs  │
│       into a directory already early in PATH.                                │
│  • Build: make -j$(nproc) sqlite3                                            │
│  • Install: make install                                                     │
│  • Verify: which sqlite3 and sqlite3 --version, and inspect linked flags or  │
│    run gcov on generated .gcda/.gcno if running tests.                       │
│                                                                              │
│ Commands I ran (for traceability)                                            │
│                                                                              │
│  • ls -la /app /app/vendor                                                   │
│  • sha256sum -c /app/vendor/sqlite-fossil-release.tar.gz.sha256              │
│  • tar -xzf /app/vendor/sqlite-fossil-release.tar.gz -C /app                 │
│  • ls -la /app/sqlite                                                        │
│  • grep -R 'gcov|fprofile-arcs|ftest-coverage' -n /app/sqlite                │
│  • sed -n on relevant autosetup and Makefile.in fragments to capture         │
│    CFLAGS.gcov1/LDFLAGS.gcov1 and configure options                          │
│  • /app/sqlite/configure --help to confirm --gcov and other options          │
│  • checked gcc/gcov/make versions, nproc, PATH contents, and permissions     │
│    with touch tests                                                          │

│ ┃    $(CFLAGS) is problematic because it is frequently overridden when     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    invoking make, which loses things like -fPIC. So... we avoid using    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃     it directly and instead add a level of indirection.  We combine      ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃     $(CFLAGS) and $(CPPFLAGS) here because that's the way the legacy     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃     build did it and many builds rely on that. See main.mk for more      ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                 details.                                 ┃ │

│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │

│ ┃       Historical note: the pre-3.48 build only honored CPPFLAGS at       ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃   configure-time, and expanded them into the generated Makefile. There   ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    are, in that build, no uses of CPPFLAGS in the configure-expanded     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┃    Makefile. Ergo: if a client configures with CPPFLAGS=... and then     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃        explicitly passes CFLAGS=... to make, the CPPFLAGS will be        ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┃                lost. That behavior is retained in 3.48+.                 ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ SQLite source with gcov instrumentation and make the resulting sqlite3 CLI   │
│ available on PATH.\n\nSolution plan (what we implemented)\n- Extract the     │
│ provided snapshot into /app/sqlite (tarball verified).\n- Configure the      │
│ source with coverage enabled and install prefix set to /opt/venv:\n          │
│ ./configure --gcov --prefix=/opt/venv\n- Build the CLI and install:\n  make  │
│ -j$(nproc) sqlite3\n  make install\n- Run the instrumented binary against a  │
│ small SQL script to produce .gcda files, then run gcov to produce textual    │
│ coverage reports for representative sources.\n\nExecution and results\n-     │
│ Build/install\n  - configure completed with --gcov and generated Makefiles   │
│ that add -DSQLITE_COVERAGE_TEST -fprofile-arcs -ftest-coverage and link with │
│ -lgcov.\n  - make built sqlite3; link line confirmed inclusion of            │
│ gcov-related flags.\n  - make install placed the binary at                   │
│ /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3 ( /opt/venv is already early on PATH and writable      │
│ ).\n- Verification\n  - which sqlite3 -> /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3\n  -          │
│ /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3 --version -> 3.50.4 (64-bit)\n  - Coverage data files  │
│ created in /app/sqlite after running the instrumented binary:\n    - *.gcda  │
│ and .gcno (e.g., sqlite3-sqlite3.gcda, sqlite3-shell.gcda, corresponding     │
│ .gcno)\n  - gcov reports produced:\n    - sqlite3.c.gcov (amalgam) — ~18.6%  │
│ lines executed from the short run\n    - shell.c.gcov (CLI code) — ~5.4%     │
│ lines executed\n  - All expected artifacts are present:\n    - Installed     │
│ binary: /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3\n    - Coverage metadata: /app/sqlite/.gcda /  │
│ .gcno\n    - gcov reports: /app/sqlite/.gcov\n\nKey findings and lessons\n-  │
│ The source tree’s configure supports --gcov; the build system correctly sets │
│ CFLAGS/LDFLAGS for gcov (CFLAGS.gcov1, LDFLAGS.gcov1).\n- Installing into    │
│ /opt/venv was appropriate because it is writable and on PATH, so the         │
│ instrumented sqlite3 is immediately usable.\n- To generate .gcda files you   │
│ must execute the instrumented binary; small scripts yield limited coverage.  │
│ For meaningful coverage numbers run a broader exercise or the project’s test │
│ suite.\n- When running gcov, supply the correct object basename/directory    │
│ (-o) that matches the produced object names (e.g., sqlite3-sqlite3,          │
│ sqlite3-shell) — otherwise gcov cannot locate the .gcda data.\n- For         │
│ HTML/aggregate coverage reports, use lcov/genhtml on the .gcda/.gcno         │
│ data.\n\nStatus\n- Complete: SQLite was configured with gcov, built, and     │
│ installed to /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3 and is on PATH. Coverage data and gcov    │
│ textual reports were produced in /app/sqlite.'                               │

│ Summary — goal                                                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Build the pre-vendored SQLite source with gcov instrumentation and make   │
│    the resulting sqlite3 CLI available on PATH.                              │
│                                                                              │
│ Solution plan (what we implemented)                                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Extract the provided snapshot into /app/sqlite (tarball verified).        │
│  • Configure the source with coverage enabled and install prefix set to      │
│    /opt/venv: ./configure --gcov --prefix=/opt/venv                          │
│  • Build the CLI and install: make -j$(nproc) sqlite3 make install           │
│  • Run the instrumented binary against a small SQL script to produce .gcda   │
│    files, then run gcov to produce textual coverage reports for              │
│    representative sources.                                                   │
│                                                                              │
│ Execution and results                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│  • Build/install                                                             │
│     • configure completed with --gcov and generated Makefiles that add       │
│       -DSQLITE_COVERAGE_TEST -fprofile-arcs -ftest-coverage and link with    │
│       -lgcov.                                                                │
│     • make built sqlite3; link line confirmed inclusion of gcov-related      │
│       flags.                                                                 │
│     • make install placed the binary at /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3 ( /opt/venv is │
│       already early on PATH and writable ).                                  │
│  • Verification                                                              │
│     • which sqlite3 -> /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3                                 │
│     • /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3 --version -> 3.50.4 (64-bit)                     │
│     • Coverage data files created in /app/sqlite after running the           │
│       instrumented binary:                                                   │
│        • *.gcda and *.gcno (e.g., sqlite3-sqlite3.gcda, sqlite3-shell.gcda,  │
│          corresponding .gcno)                                                │
│     • gcov reports produced:                                                 │
│        • sqlite3.c.gcov (amalgam) — ~18.6% lines executed from the short run │
│        • shell.c.gcov (CLI code) — ~5.4% lines executed                      │
│     • All expected artifacts are present:                                    │
│        • Installed binary: /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3                             │
│        • Coverage metadata: /app/sqlite/*.gcda / *.gcno                      │
│        • gcov reports: /app/sqlite/*.gcov                                    │
│                                                                              │
│ Key findings and lessons                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│  • The source tree’s configure supports --gcov; the build system correctly   │
│    sets CFLAGS/LDFLAGS for gcov (CFLAGS.gcov1, LDFLAGS.gcov1).               │
│  • Installing into /opt/venv was appropriate because it is writable and on   │
│    PATH, so the instrumented sqlite3 is immediately usable.                  │
│  • To generate .gcda files you must execute the instrumented binary; small   │
│    scripts yield limited coverage. For meaningful coverage numbers run a     │
│    broader exercise or the project’s test suite.                             │
│  • When running gcov, supply the correct object basename/directory (-o) that │
│    matches the produced object names (e.g., sqlite3-sqlite3, sqlite3-shell)  │
│    — otherwise gcov cannot locate the .gcda data.                            │
│  • For HTML/aggregate coverage reports, use lcov/genhtml on the .gcda/.gcno  │
│    data.                                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│ Status                                                                       │
│                                                                              │
│  • Complete: SQLite was configured with gcov, built, and installed to        │
│    /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3 and is on PATH. Coverage data and gcov textual      │
│    reports were produced in /app/sqlite.                                     │
